ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u17	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I would like to present to you a draft law amending the budget law for 2021. The original draft budget law for 2021 was drawn up in the middle of last year, i.e. in a situation of high uncertainty not only with regard to the projected trends in the economy in the current year, but also with regard to the macroeconomic situation and expected trends in the following quarters of 2020. At that time, it was extremely difficult to predict the final scale and structure of the collapse of economic activity caused by the outbreak of the COVID pandemic. This made it difficult to predict the level of real gross domestic product in the second half of 2020, especially as there was a large uncertainty about the continuation of the pandemic. This high uncertainty has translated into a very cautious preparation of forecasts, especially with regard to the dynamics of economic activity in 2020. However, economic performance in the second half of 2020 and in the first half of 2021, including Thanks to stronger fiscal support, they have proved to be much better than expectations, despite the fact that during this period there have been two more waves of pandemics, which have temporarily led to stronger administrative restrictions aimed at reducing the spread of pandemics. In the fourth quarter of 2020, GDP fell by a quarter to a quarter of 0,3%, but already in the first quarter of 2021 the quarter by a quarter increased by 1.3%. Today, we already know that only in the first half of 2021, GDP has increased by 5% in real terms by one year to one year. Real GDP is projected to increase by 4.9% throughout 2021 as compared to an increase of 4% planned in the draft budget law and budget law for 2021. The macroeconomic situation presented this year is much more favourable from the point of view of the budgetary revenue collection process. The positive impact of economic recovery is particularly evident in tax revenues. This category of revenue of the state budget can ultimately be higher by up to PLN 55.7 billion, i.e. by more than 15% , from the originally planned amounts. In view of the above, given the better macro-economic situation in Poland and the increased budgetary revenue, it is crucial that our budgetary policy can also serve as a tool to further support and stimulate economic growth. The main figures in the revision of the State budget for the current year would be as follows. Following the implementation of the amendment, which I am presenting herein, the projected total revenue of the state budget in 2021 would amount to PLN 483 billion, i.e. by PLN 78,5 billion more than was foreseen in the original budget law. It is worth noting that tax revenues in this year can amount to up to PLN 424.8 billion. In addition to tax revenues there will also be higher revenues from CO2 auctions. There was also a higher contribution from the profit of NBP as well as dividends. The increased revenue generated by the State is planned to be used not only to significantly reduce the maximum deficit planned in the Budget Act, but also to increase expenditure in specific expenditure areas. Therefore, the limit set in the draft amendment to the Budget Act for the year 2021 is PLN 523.5 billion, which means that it will be higher than that planned in the original Budget Act by more than PLN 36.7 billion. What would be the main expenditure groups in the revision of the budget law? This is primarily the support of local government units in carrying out their own tasks as part of a comprehensive mechanism to stabilise and strengthen the finances of local government units in connection with the implementation of the Polish Load programme; to increase the expenditure on support for local authorities in the implementation of investments mainly on municipal and district roads; to allocate additional funds to the development of modern and ecological forms of rail and transport; additional funds for the continuation of the process of modernising and increasing the operational capacity of the Armed Forces, for the ongoing functioning and investment tasks of police, Border Guard and State Fire Departments; to transfer additional funds to the incentive fund for employees of the budgetary sphere; additional support for people with disabilities through the power of RARS and PFRON; to increase resources for higher education and science institutions to carry out investment tasks; to increase expenditure on financing of cultural, agricultural and hard coal mining tasks. The proposed project found that the deficit at 31 December 2021 would be no more than PLN 40.5 billion, i.e. as much as PLN 41.8 billion less than the budget law for 2021. Ladies and gentlemen! Given the current macroeconomic situation and the possible negative situation for the economy, the consequences of the ongoing COVID pandemic, the Council of Ministers decided to adopt a proposal to amend this year's budget. This would allow additional funding to be provided, inter alia. on investment and development tasks in order to further stimulate the economy and mitigate the negative effects of the pandemic. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Today's meeting, together with the draft amendment to the budget law and the self-correction, will also consider the so-called draft. a budget law. The main solutions contained in this project relate to changes in remuneration in the public finance sector. In place of institutional maintenance of the level of remuneration, it is proposed to introduce a mechanism to increase remuneration after obtaining the approval of the Minister or another supervisory authority and the approval of the Minister responsible for public finances. The Around Budget Act also provides for the creation of an additional 6-percent incentive fund for employees of budgetary units. Here I must say that in 2021 the so-called was not planned. The 3 per cent prize fund and these actions in some way compensate for what was not granted to employees due to the very prudent approach at the beginning of this year in the Budget Act. It is also necessary to refer to the situation of 2020 when administrative decisions were blocked by measures, including wage appropriations. The draft law also proposes solutions that implement government announcements in the area of local government units and their investment tasks. This mainly concerns the support of local government units with additional resources of PLN 4 billion, it is a kind of investment subsidy for water and sewerage tasks. So in addition to PLN 8 billion, which I already had the honour to inform here in this Chamber when discussing the income law of local government units, local government units in this year would receive PLN 4 billion for investment tasks in the area of water and sewers. In order to stimulate and further maintain the country's dynamic economic development and improve the state of the road network, including local infrastructure, it is also appropriate to increase the flow of funds from the state budget to the government road fund. One of the government's priorities is pro-family policy and, within the framework of it, action to develop a childcare system for children up to 3 years of age, including through the "Milch+" programme. It is therefore proposed to extend until 30 November 2022 the deadline for the use of expenditure incurred by the Labour Fund for the "Milch+' programme. The project also provides for additional support from the state budget of special funds and other entities to carry out health, public safety, fire protection, national defence, antiquities, as well as initiatives for people with disabilities. In addition, support is envisaged for railway infrastructure managers to maintain railway infrastructure renovations and capital investments. Support for the implementation of investment processes would also be provided through the Around Budget Act and the provisions of the Budgetary Act of universities and institutes operating in the Research Network of Łukasiewicz. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the provisions presented in the draft budget law, the self amendment and the around budget law, I ask the High House to accept them in the proposed wording. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

GDP. It's about the entire debt, not just the debt of the Treasury. As far as the issue of local government units is concerned, here we have draft budget law and around budget law, in which the revenue of local government units increases by PLN 12 billion. 8 billion is a general reserve. Local government units will decide at their discretion to use these funds. 4 billion will also be divided in a statutory, algorithmic manner. It will reach the local government units, which have the biggest deficiencies in water and sewage infrastructure. It will only have to be used for this purpose, for this infrastructure, at its own discretion. There is no need for any own contribution, the funds will be passed on by law. There was also a question about prizes in the local budgetary sphere. This 8 billion PLN could be earmarked for any purpose by local government units, but this incentive fund was rebuilt because it was the staff of the budgetary sphere in 2020 that had blocked the prize funds, and in 2021 this fund was not created. The situation has improved so much that we can rebuild this fund. As regards the issue of government agencies, because there was also a question about ARiMR, the draft around budget law also allows for an increase in the funds for remuneration. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with the examination procedure referred to in paragraph 1 of this Article. 2, making a new sound. Another amendment: in the article. 1 in point 1, delete Article. 117c and Art. 1 in point 1 in Article. 117d paragraph. 3 - also make a new wording on the issues of the majority when adopting an emergency. I will briefly inform the High House that there was a debate in the committee, in particular, on the matter of the ordinary majority, which the Rules of Procedure introduce as regards the extension of the state of emergency. Here I would like to present some information to the High Sejm that was on the committee. It began with a dispute about the interpretation of the Article. 230 and 231 Constitutions which require voting on the President's regulation on the introduction of an emergency state, namely that the Sejm may repeal this regulation by an absolute majority in the presence of at least half of the statutory number of Members. In contrast, in the article. 230 The Constitution provides that an extension of the state of emergency may take place only once with the approval of the Sejm and for a period of not more than 60 days. Therefore, the Constitution does not specify the majority to be voted on on the issue of extending the state of emergency. And since that's not there, we're referring to the article. 120 Constitution, which provides that the Sejm shall adopt laws by simple majority in the presence of at least half of the statutory number of Members, unless the Constitution provides for a different majority or resolution or the Sejm Act provides otherwise. So in this case, the amendment is designed to regulate the introduction of emergency conditions at all and how the vote on the extension of the emergency status is to be held. And there is a proposal made by Members and adopted by the committee that this extension is to be voted by a simple majority, which is in line with the principles set out in the Constitution. In this respect, there was a dispute in the committee and the committee decided that we were on this project, which was proposed by a group of Members. In fact, the issue was still concerned with repealing or extending the emergency situation during the debate. There is a proposal to exclude the possibility of submitting or to exclude the possibility of submitting formal applications. There was also a discussion and a dispute in the committee in this regard. However, the Commission has decided to adopt this solution, which is proposed by the applicants, in order to exclude the possibility of submitting formal applications in this respect. One of the arguments which I also put forward in this regard and which was raised by the applicants is that an extension of the state of emergency or emergency may take place, for example. on the last day of this state, and then the submission of formal applications could lead to the Sejm not being able to give its consent to the extension and that would have ended. Then it is no longer possible to extend the condition that is completed. The renewal procedure should therefore be re-launched. The Commission accepted such an argument and accepted this solution, which the Commission is asking the High Sejm to accept. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

That's one thing. Secondly, a very important and important issue that needs to be said at the beginning of my speech is that I have not met such a number of speeches, invectives, allegations regarding what the Ministry of Education and Science has prepared in the form of so-called. the freedom package. I am really amazed and surprised because I am not - as was pointed out here or accused at the committee meeting - a proponent of a pessimistic view of the nature of man or a proponent of moral pessimism. No, it's the other way around. I assume that you are all looking with great enthusiasm at what we call university autonomy, and with great attachment you stand by what we call freedom - what I am convinced of - but perhaps for reasons of one or another, but mostly it seems that - this is my private - ideological opinion. You do not want to see a problem and a phenomenon that is present at Polish universities, Polish universities and universities. Please bear in mind that we are talking here about a freedom package that will apply not only to universities and schools with research status, but also to those that carry out ordinary teaching activities at the level of first degree studies. Firstly, I would like to clarify that the definition of freedom, which is an important and important definition from the point of view of this amendment, is freedom defined once by liberals, still in the 19th century, which is classic - in the sense of Isaiah Berlin - freedom in a negative sense. So it is about creating boundaries of law, extending far-reaching boundaries that allow for freedom of expression. Freedom of expression and freedom of expression in the case of a publicist, freedom of expression and expression in the case of a politician is linked with responsibility for the words spoken. We know there's a rule committee, we know there's an ethics committee, we know there's civil liability. or even criminal in connection with what we say. As far as the university is concerned, as far as research institutes are concerned, let us remember that freedom here is something important and fundamental and fundamental. First of all, the choice of the subject of the study, secondly, the choice of the method or methodology of research leading to scientific inquiry, to carry out scientific research. This freedom package guarantees every scientist that, on the basis of a methodology appropriate to discipline or this research area... the researcher has the right to choose this methodology. I talked about this at the committee meeting, and I'm gonna say it again. When it comes to social sciences and humanities, including legal sciences, it is proven, and in scientific terms, that in every scientific procedure is what we call value. Thus, value is inevitable at every stage of scientific activity. Thus, by introducing a provision saying that no one, because of their system of values, has a system of religious or philosophical beliefs, can bear responsibility for this, we recognize that everyone has the right to carry out scientific research in accordance with his own philosophical or religious view. Ladies and gentlemen, since you are so eager to refer to the liberals - if you do not mention Wolter's name - please remember that the freedom package gives everyone freedom of expression. It gives it to all those who are in the area of the academic community, because this law speaks very clearly about academic space and freedom of participation and requires the rector to support such initiatives, conduct debates. That's one thing. So we are not dealing in this law with any narrowing down what we call freedom, freedom in this field of research. Of course, it is also worth appealing to various studies related to methodological issues or to the metascience sphere. Everywhere it is emphasized that the basis for the development of science and reaching the truth in all teachings - not only those I have spoken of: humanistic, social or other, but also strict - is freedom, is also what we call revolution. The name of Copernicus was mentioned, but please also remember these moments very important and important, such as the concept of classical dynamics, and later also what is related to classical mechanics or later quantum mechanics. These are really revolutions. Thomas Kuhn wrote that the foundation of scientific development is the scientific revolution. Hence, it is completely unclear to me why these ideas related to allegations which are so slanderously directed at the address of either the Ministry of Education and Science or Minister Czarnek. Ladies and gentlemen! As for this committee - because it also appeared in many questions - I would like to make you aware that it already exists, always existed and was treated as an appeal institution, so everyone had the right to appeal against these resolutions or those decisions that took place at university level. This amendment therefore does not introduce a new committee. If this committee is here, it's a legal solution for her. What is more, I repeat once again: she performs functions only and only appeals. Hence the question of why there is no cassation action here and why this is about the Court of Appeal in Warsaw. Because this is already concrete in the current law and the Ministry did not want to make any changes here. So you might ask: What is the freedom package for then, so it guarantees freedom in all dimensions? ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u259	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the government draft law amending the Law on the protection of animals used for scientific or educational purposes, prints 1454 and 1551. The project on which the joint committees were working is an amendment to the Law of 15 January 2015 on the protection of animals used for scientific or educational purposes. The need to adopt this law is due to the initiation by the European Commission of formal proceedings against Poland in relation to the way in which the provisions of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 22 September 2010 on the protection of animals used for scientific purposes are transposed into national law. It is possible to remedy the shortcomings that have been identified by amending this law. The draft amendment also takes into account the need to adapt the Law to the regulations contained in the Law of 20 July 2018 - Law on Higher Education and Science, as well as to the conditions and social needs indicated in the justification. As the name of the amended law itself indicates, its purpose is to protect animals used for scientific purposes. Although the wording itself of the animal used is now pejorative, the assumptions accompanying the legislative work focus not only on clarifying the rules in a formal way, one which will not raise any question of interpretation, but above all on eliminating the various types of abuse and problems identified, among others. under the control of the Supreme Audit Chamber. We are all aware that we are obliged to provide the right conditions for the existence of animals. We meet with voices saying that man should not use nature in this way. The word "used," often mistakenly associated with abuse or other cruelty, naturally causes outrage. However, let us remember that the huge scientific, research progress that we observe on a daily basis is possible, among others. by the participation of animals. I will quote the catechism of the Catholic Church: The animals, as well as plants and inanimate beings, are by nature destined for the benefit of the common humanity in the past, now and in the future. The use of natural, plant and animal resources in the world cannot be separated from respect for moral requirements. The planned changes aim to ensure that methods are used more effectively to achieve the objectives, experience without the use of animals, reduce to a minimum the number of animals used in the procedures, improve breeding, maintenance, care and procedures so as to eliminate or minimise potential pain, suffering, districtes or permanent damage to the organism. This law is a continuous improvement of the so-called. 3R principle contained in three words: replacement, limitation, improvement. The Law and Justice Club, given that the proposed amendments to the law have been positively assessed by the joint committees, will vote in favour of this law. However, after the discussion and following many requests, suggestions, on behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I submit to your Marshal an amendment to the bill along with their justification. This proposal aims to transpose into national law the provisions of Directive 2010/63/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 22 September 2010 on the protection of animals used for scientific purposes. The proposed amendments aim to complement the existing solutions and to clarify the rules that raise doubts about interpretation and create difficulties in applying the law. Such changes must also be made due to the results of the audit carried out by the Supreme Audit Chamber in March 2017. The proposed amendments aim to improve the protection of animals used for scientific or educational purposes. They are also to check the functioning of the regulatory system for the use of animals for those purposes, including the process of obtaining an entry in the register of breeders, suppliers and users and obtaining consent to carry out experiments on animals. The most important solutions are, as the applicants present, the extension of the scope of the 3R principle, which aims to protect animals used for scientific or educational purposes, in order to ensure more effectively the use of methods that enable the achievement of the objectives of the experiment without the use of animals, to reduce to a minimum the number of animals used in procedures without prejudice to the purposes of those procedures, to improve the breeding, maintenance, care and methods used in procedures so that animals are eliminated or reduced to a minimum of potential pain, suffering, districting or permanent damage to the organism. The Act is intended to ensure that Polish teams are active in the field. animal welfare has implemented the obligation to inform staff of current technical and scientific developments related to the application of the 3R principle and to provide advice on the socialisation of animals for adoption. It is also intended to require breeders, suppliers and users to establish a programme for finding a new home, or in the case of wild animals, a rehabilitation programme by restoring them to a natural habitat. It is also intended to amend the definition of breeder in order to allow the entry in the register of livestock farmers used in experiments, including livestock keepers used in procedures, to clarify the rules governing exceptions to the application of anaesthetics and painkillers, to supplement the rules laying down the conditions for handling animals after being used in procedures, to introduce the possibility of returning the livestock to a place of farming or farming, to ensure that these procedures are not carried out on animals if a different method or testing strategy is legally recognised in the Union to achieve appropriate results. If you read these records that have appeared there, you will first of all see that the supervision and control of research, on animal experiments through this amendment are much stronger than ever. Firstly, the amendment sets up animal welfare teams. This is the first point that did not appear at all in these speeches by Members of the Left. Secondly, the powers of the district veterinarian to stop and prevent the continuation of the studies if there is a breach of those rules and principles as laid down in the plan, in a project approved by the local ethics committee. It's the county doctor who has the authority. It seems to be a very strong impulse or such a blocking moment, preventing abuse of these activities or experiments. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u417	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Joint Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Energy, Climate and State Assets on the draft law amending the Act - Environmental Law and the Act on greenhouse gas emission management and other substances from print No 1514. The project entered the Sejm on August 30, 2 September was sent to these committees for their first reading. This took place on 15 September, after which the committees carried out detailed work on the project, which resulted in the report in print 1553 being presented here. The draft law implements the provisions, first, of Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 2018/1135 of 10 August 2018 establishing the type, format and frequency of transmission of information to be made available by Member States for reports on the implementation of Directive 2010/75/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council on industrial emissions. Secondly, it implements the provisions of Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 2019/1741 of 23 September 2019 determining the format and frequency of the transmission of data to be made available by Member States for reporting under Regulation (EC) No 166/2006 of the European Parliament and of the Council on the establishment of a European Pollution Release and Transfer Register and amending Council Directive 91/689/EEC and 96/61/EC. The new reporting rules require Member States to report two separate reports. First, reports containing the identification data of industrial plants covered by Directive 2010/75/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council on industrial emissions and Regulation 166/2006, which feeds the EU Registry established by Commission Decision No 1135 of 2018 referred to above. Secondly, the second report to be submitted by the Member State is an integrated report which contains data on releases and transfers of pollutants or waste and emission data from large combustion plants. Thus, Member States are also required to provide the European Commission with data on establishments which did not indicate that the thresholds for releases and transfers of pollutants were exceeded in the reporting year in question and only carried out at least one of those activities set out in Annex I to Regulation (EC) No 166/2006 of the European Parliament and of the Council on the establishment of a European Register for the Release and Transfer of Pollution. In addition, the new European Union legislation reduced the deadline for Member States to report to the European Commission from 15 months to 11 months after the end of the reporting year, and, following the amendment, the operators - and these are the standards that we introduce into Polish law - will report to the Regional Environmental Protection Officer on exceeding threshold values, as well as the data necessary to identify the plant by 31 March of the year following the reporting year. By 31 August of the year following the reporting year, the Regional Environmental Inspector will forward these data to the Chief Environmental Inspector. All these model reports, detailed data, technical conditions, form and way of transmission will be set out in the Regulation of the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs. the climate for which authorisation is included in the Act. It should be noted here that Annex III to Regulation (EC) No 166/2006 is repealed in European legislation and the format for the transmission of data to the European Commission is laid down by Commission Implementing Decision. On the other hand, the article was repealed. 16 Regulation (EC) No 166/2006, which required Member States to submit 3-year reports on the implementation of that Regulation. This obligation to submit 3-year reports is removed from Polish legislation. High Izbo! All these changes in EU legislation are introduced into the Polish legal order just discussed here by me draft law. In addition, print project 1514 refers to criminal rules concerning the timely fulfilment of reporting obligations by national entities. The penalties currently in force are disproportionate to the seriousness of the infringements. Currently, e.g. the administrative penalty for failure to report within the deadline on the National Register for Release and Transfer of Pollution shall be 50,000. zł, whether the delay was day, week, month or longer. On the other hand, the penalty for the transmission of incomplete, inconsistent or unreliable data is 5 000. zł, also regardless of the gravity of the infringement. The provisions of the bill discussed here eliminate these shortcomings in terms of non-balance of penalties and excessively severe penalties. The amendments to the rules on administrative penalties are as follows: first, a sanction shall be imposed for failure to provide identification data by the operator, i.e. an administrative penalty of 5 000. zł - this is a new penalty, because the obligation to provide identification data is a new obligation, but subsequent changes are already very in favour of the obliged entities; secondly, administrative penalties for delays in the transmission of data exceeding threshold values for releases and transfers of pollutants or waste will amount to PLN 200 per day of delay, but for no more than 365 days of delay this penalty can be charged, so the penalty will be proportionate to the seriousness of the infringements; third, there will be a penalty for not ensuring the quality of the data transmitted, which will be between PLN 500 and 5 000. zł, i.e. it will be possible to weigh this administrative penalty depending on the type of infringement and not preserving the proper quality of the data transmitted; fourthly, it will be possible to waive administrative penalties - this possibility has not yet been possible. High Izbo! The amendments to the national rules which result from the entry into force of the new provisions of European Union law are the following: firstly, the waiver of the deadline for the transmission of the report by GIOŚ to the European Commission; secondly, the shortening of the deadline for the assessment of the quality of the data transmitted by the operators, and the transfer of those data to the upper floor, i.e. the General Environmental Inspector; third, the abolition, as I have already mentioned, but for the sake of order, of the obligation to submit a 3-year report by the General Environmental Inspector to the European Commission; and fourthly, the obligation to provide identification data to the establishment in which the installation listed in Annex I to Regulation (EC) No 166/2006 is carried out, with information on the exceeding of the performance thresholds, whether or not the operator finds that the thresholds for releases and transmission of pollutants are exceeded during a given reporting year, are to be met by all establishments with adequate efficiency. High Izbo! The amendments proposed in this Act align national provisions with the revised European Union rules, but also allow for reasonable and fair treatment of entities required to provide information in this area. High Izbo! Art. 1 of the draft contains amendments to the Act - Environmental Law, Art. 2 in turn includes amendments to the Law on the greenhouse gas emission management system and other substances, Article. 3 and Article 4 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [3] shall apply mutatis mutandis. 4 contain transitional provisions and Article. 5 provides that the law shall enter into force 3 months after the date of the announcement, i.e. the vacathio legis, is relatively long in order for all obliged entities to prepare for this. High Izbo! The Joint Committees: Environmental Protection, Natural Resources and Forestry and Energy, Climate and State Assets adopted a report on the draft law by 42 votes in favour, with no votes against and with three abstentions. Therefore, on behalf of both committees, I ask the High House to adopt the bill contained in the report of the Joint Committees in print No 1553. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u449	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present my position on behalf of the Law and Justice Club with regard to the government draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws. The original draft is in print 1513 and the committee report is in print 1565. The aim of the project is to include in the national legal order the necessary changes following the entry into force of European Union rules on capital requirements for investment firms, so-called. IFD/IFR package. It concerns the implementation of Directive 2019/2034 of the European Parliament and of the Council on prudential supervision of investment firms, amending also previous EU regulations in this area. It also concerns the implementation of Regulation 2033 of the European Parliament and of the Council of the same year on prudential requirements for investment firms, amending also previous EU regulations in this field. This legislation is another attempt to improve the functioning of the credit and investment area following the guidelines of the Basel Committee on Banking Supervision. The main intention of regulation is to define requirements for the protection of depositors, but also of all taxpayers, against the possibility of bankruptcy of credit institutions and loans in the event of a possible deconuncture. Investment firms are divided into three categories related to business scale and their mutual relations and capital links. The first category is the largest, systemically important companies. The second category is companies that do not generate systemic risk, but generate risks for customers - possible risks associated with bankruptcy. Finally, the third category is small and non-capitalised companies. Investment firms will be required to meet a fixed minimum capital requirement at any time. They will also have to have an internal control and monitoring system in relation to the capital requirements formulated. The proposed implementation requires changes in the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments, in Bank Law, in the Act on Pre-existing Lists and mortgage banks, in the Act on the Functioning of Cooperative Banks and in many other laws. The project offers opportunities for better regulation of credit and investment activities for two different quality institutions, with different business models and a different range of services offered. Once the new law is adopted and the appropriate prudential arrangements are taken into account, the administrative burden will be better aligned with the business. The regulation will avoid excessive costs which could jeopardise the viability of certain economic operators in this segment. The Law and Justice Club supports this project and requests that it be further examined. The regulations contained in the project do not affect the activities of micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. By requesting further proceding and expressing a positive opinion on the government project, at the same time, on behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I make two additional amendments to your Marshals asking for their opinion by the Committee on Public Finance. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u452	Thank you very much, Member. Please, Mr Jan Szopiński, Left.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u453	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour of presenting our position on behalf of the club Lewis to the government draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws. The aim of this project is to bring our national law into line with EU law on capital requirements for investment firms, and in this respect the main objective of the IFD/IFR package is to establish a new prudential regime for investment firms. The Act brings three main assumptions regarding the new prudential regime for investment firms. Thus, investment firms are divided into three categories due to their size and interrelationship with other financial and economic entities. The first category includes investment firms in the systemic sense, the largest and most interlinked, which are still subject to the existing prudential framework resulting from the Regulation. The second category includes entities that do not generate systemic risk, but which have the highest probability of generating risks for market customers or for the proper functioning of investment firms. They should therefore be subject to certain prudential requirements adapted to specific risks. The third category is small and interconnected investment firms for which the least restrictive requirements are laid down. The second element of the prudential regime for investment firms which, due to their size and interrelationship with other financial and economic entities are not considered to be systemically important entities, will apply to any investment firm in order to facilitate the application of prudential requirements for investment firms in the European Union that are part of banking groups, and in this respect the capital requirements of investment firms will depend on the type of activity, the services on which the investment firm is authorised to provide or operate. Finally, the third assumption, i.e. taking into account the higher risk faced by investment firms of the second category, so the minimum own funds requirement for such firms will correspond to their fixed minimum capital requirement of 1/4 of their fixed indirect costs for the previous year or the sum of their requirements on the basis of sets of risk factors tailored to the specificities of investment firms by which capital is determined for the risks occurring in certain areas of investment firms' activities, whichever is the higher. Finally, investment firms will be required to comply with liquidity requirements, which will result in an obligation to have internal procedures to monitor and manage liquidity requirements. In addition, they will be required to maintain at any time at least 1/3 of the requirement for fixed indirect costs in liquid assets. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Left Club is in favour of further proceding this project in order to adapt our national law to the EU's capital requirements for Polish investment firms. Thank you very much.

They will set up, among others. the rules for the estimation of internal capital at the brokerage house are adapted to the Directive, inter alia. by indicating that internal capital should also include liquid assets. Furthermore, provisions not provided for in the Directive are repealed for the assessment of internal capital at consolidated level where the brokerage house is dependent on holding companies of a certain nature. Furthermore, it does not provide for an obligation to prepare a supervisory programme and conduct stress tests. The draft also amends the provision on remuneration policy in brokerage homes. Notwithstanding these measures, in order to ensure that cyclical economic downturns do not lead to a breach of capital requirements or jeopardise the ability of the brokerage house to liquidate and cease its activities in an orderly manner, the KNF will have the additional power to impose a higher internal capital requirement on the brokerage house. Following the introduction of liquidity requirements for brokerage houses, KNF will also be entitled to impose higher liquidity requirements on the house in justified cases. All supervisory measures of the KNF will be taken in the form of administrative decisions. In order to ensure appropriate supervisory powers, KNF will be able to request additional information on prudential requirements beyond the reporting obligations set out in the package. In violation of the IFR Regulation, sanctions of up to 21 000 are at stake. zł. The package assumes that investment firms are divided into three categories due to their size and interrelationship with other financial, economic entities. Under the new prudential regime, the principle is to be introduced that the requirements for the initial capital level of an investment firm will depend on the type of activity for which the investment firm is authorised to provide or operate. In addition, investment firms will be required to demonstrate compliance at any time with a fixed minimum capital requirement equal to the level of the initial capital required. In the case of small and unrelated investment firms, they will be required to have own funds equal to their fixed minimum capital requirement or 1/4 of their fixed indirect costs calculated on the basis of their activities in the previous year, whichever is the higher. In addition, investment firms will be required to comply with liquidity requirements, which will result in the obligation to have internal procedures to monitor and manage liquidity requirements. There will also be an obligation to disclose relevant information, e.g. on own funds and liquidity requirements. High Izbo! EUR, currently 125 000. the euro, except where the brokerage house does not operate an alternative trading venue or does not perform operations involving the holding or recording of financial instruments and the keeping of cash accounts. Therefore, these requirements are slightly increasing, while during the consultation, as I have already mentioned, concerns were not raised by the market. As regards Mr Szopiński's question, it is worth noting that when it comes to warnings, UKNF is under supervision. The task of the Ministry and the Government is primarily to create regulations that such supervision can achieve. It is worth pointing out several legal provisions that have been introduced in the last few decades, actually counting precisely. Regarding stricte to GetBack, but these provisions resonate to other areas, a minimum value of one nominal subordinated bond was introduced, which cannot be less than 400 000. zł. This means that retail investors have a blocked opportunity to enter such securities, because retail investors do not fall into securities with a minimum value of 400 000. zł. First of all, UKNF has recently been strengthened, i.e. the entity which has the task of warning potential clients on the one hand, but on the other hand it also supervises the entities or parasites that operate such practices. We also provide cooperation between UKNF and entities such as ABW, CBA, National Tax Administration, Police and UOKiK President. Finally, as far as the practice of such contracts is concerned, we have introduced an obligation for investment firms to record conversations and record in electronic correspondence for portfolio management services consisting of one or more financial instruments and investment advisory services. We've eliminated the so-called. the practice of crawling private offers which did not require the preparation and approval of the tender document in KNF. This is the practice taken out of the GetBack business. We have also introduced the personal responsibility of the members of the management board or the supervisory board of the company when the company has committed infringements of the laws governing the activities of such an entity. Finally, we introduced a cyclical reporting requirement to KNF by investment firms, banks and trust banks for information on products sold to customers by these entities in order to enable UKNF to monitor the market with a view to identifying risks and new trends in the financial market. So that these are the actions aimed at ensuring that the situation with GetBack, both on an individual and subject-to-case basis, is not repeated, because I will remind you that the GetBack affair has resulted from the activities in question, i.e. offering retail financial instruments adversely to customers. As regards Mr Gawkowski's cybersecurity question, I would like to inform you that the European Union is currently at the stage of designing and consulting the so-called. DORA regulations. It is intended to regulate the scope of cybersecurity throughout the European Union market, i.e. in terms of both subject matter and subject matter. I think that the Commission will soon be finalising this regulation and, to the extent it is necessary, we will also implement it in the Polish legal order. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u467	Position of the Parliamentary Circle Poland 2050 on the government draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws, print No 1513. The aim of the project is to make the necessary changes to the national legal order following the entry into force of European Union rules on capital requirements for investment firms, so-called. an IFD/IFR package that includes: - Directive (EU) 2019/2034 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 November 2019 on the prudential supervision of investment firms and amending Directives 2002/87/EC, 2009/65/EC, 2011/61/EU, 2013/36/EU, 2014/59/EU and 2014/65/EU Urz. EU L 314, 05.12.2019, p. 64, late. zm.), hereinafter referred to as the IFD Directive, - Regulation (EU) 2019/2033 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 November 2019 on prudential requirements for investment firms and amending Regulations (EU) No 1093/2010, (EU) No 575/2013, (EU) No 600/2014 and (EU) No 806/2014 Urz. EU L 314, 05.12.2019, p. One, late. zm.), hereinafter referred to as the IFR Regulation. The aim of the IFD/IFR package is to establish a new prudential regime for investment firms. The prudential supervision of investment firms should take into account the ability of investment firms and their clients to take risks and the different degrees of that risk. It should also help avoid imposing excessive administrative burdens on investment firms. At the same time, it should be possible to strike a balance between ensuring the safety of investment firms' customers and avoiding excessive costs which could jeopardise the viability of their business. Key assumptions of the new prudential regime for investment firms included in the IFD/IFR package: Investment firms are divided into three categories due to their size and their interrelationship with other financial and economic entities. The first category includes systemically important investment firms, the largest and most interlinked, which are still subject to the existing prudential framework under the CRR Regulation and the CRD (necessity to obtain authorisation as a credit institution). The second category includes entities that do not generate systemic risk, but have the highest probability of generating risks to customers, markets or the proper functioning of investment firms. They should therefore be subject to certain prudential requirements adapted to specific risks. The third category is small and interconnected investment firms for which the least restrictive requirements are laid down. At the same time, mechanisms are envisaged for monitoring companies in order to meet the requirements on which they are classified into a given category (groups). The prudential supervision system for investment firms which, due to their size and interrelationship with other financial and economic entities are not considered to be systemically important entities, will apply to each investment firm. In order to facilitate the application of prudential requirements for investment firms in the EU that are part of banking groups (avoidance of distortions of certain business models whose risks are already covered by prudential rules), such investment firms will be able to obtain supervisory decisions on their application of the requirements of the CRR and the CRD. The requirements for the initial capital level of the investment firm will depend on the type of activity (services) for which the investment firm is authorised to provide or operate. The structure of the investment firm's own funds will be regulated by the relevant application of the provisions of the CRR. Investment firms will be required to demonstrate compliance at any time with a fixed minimum capital requirement equal to the level of the initial capital required. Small and unrelated investment firms will be required to have own funds equal to their fixed minimum capital requirement or one quarter of their fixed indirect costs calculated on the basis of their activities in the previous year, whichever is the higher. In order to take into account the higher risks to which Category 2 investment firms are exposed, the minimum own funds requirement for such firms will correspond to their fixed minimum capital requirement, one quarter of their fixed indirect costs for the previous year or the sum of their requirements on the basis of a set of risk factors tailored to the specificities of investment firms (K factors), by which capital is determined for the risks occurring in certain areas of investment firms' activities, whichever is the higher. Investment firms will be required to comply with liquidity requirements, which will result in an obligation to have internal procedures to monitor and manage liquidity requirements. In addition, they will be required to maintain at any time at least one third of the requirement for fixed indirect costs in liquid assets. In the case of investment firms in groups, prudential consolidation rules were foreseen, including for groups consisting exclusively of investment firms, a group capital test for simpler group structures. Responsible for the activities of investment firms and prudential requirements for reporting to investment firms, regarding the level and structure of their own funds, their own funds requirements, the basis for calculating their own funds requirements, their business profile and the size of the parameters used for the purposes of recognising investment firms as small and unrelated and other investment firms, Obligation to disclose relevant information, e.g. on own funds and liquidity requirements. Salary policy requirements to reduce the risk of business. Principles of cooperation between EU competent authorities, rules on ensuring supervisory and sanctioning powers for competent authorities and rules on the conduct of audit and supervisory evaluation of investment firms. It is therefore necessary to amend the following acts: - the Act of 29 July 2005 on trading in financial instruments (OJ 2005 L 140, p. OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 328, late. zm.), - Law of 29 August 1997 - Bank law (Journal of Laws No. Member of the Commission 1896, late. zm.), - the Law of 29 August 1997 on pledge lists and mortgage banks (Journal of Laws No. Member of the Commission 415), - the Act of 7 December 2000 on the functioning of cooperative banks, their association and the association banks (Journal of Laws No. OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 102, late. zm.), - Law of 28 February 2003 - Bankruptcy Law (Journal of Laws). Member of the Commission 1228, late. zm.), - the Act of 14 March 2003 on Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego (Journal of Laws). Member of the Commission 1198, late. zm.), - the Investment Funds and Alternative Investment Fund Management Act of 27 May 2004 (Journal of Laws). OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 605), - the Act of 29 July 2005 on the supervision of the capital market (Journal of Laws No. Member of the Commission 1400, late. zm.), - the Act of 29 July 2005 on Public Offering and Conditions for Introducing Financial Instruments to Organized Trading and Public Companies (Journal of Laws). Member of the Commission 2080, late. zm.), - the Law of 21 July 2006 on the supervision of the financial market (Journal of Laws No. Member of the Commission 2059, late. zm.), - the Act of 5 November 2009 on cooperative savings and credit banks (Journal of Laws). Member of the Commission 1643, late. zm.), - the Law of 12 February 2010 on the recapitalisation of certain institutions and on government financial stabilisation instruments. U. z 2018 poz. 124, late. zm.), - the Payment Services Act of 19 August 2011 (Journal of Laws No. Member of the Commission 794, late. zm.), - the bond law of 15 January 2015 (Journal of Laws No. Member of the Commission 1208, late. zm.), - Law of 15 May 2015 - Restructuring Law (Journal of Laws No. Member of the Commission 814, late. zm.), - the Law of 5 August 2015 on macro-prudential supervision of the financial system and crisis management in the financial system (Journal of Laws No. OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 140, late. zm.), - Act of 11 September 2015 on insurance and reinsurance activities (Journal of Laws No. OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 1130), - the Act of 10 June 2016 on the Bank Guarantee Fund, the Deposit Insurance Scheme and compulsory restructuring (Journal of Laws). Member of the Commission 842, late. zm.), - Law of 15 December 2017 on insurance distribution (Journal of Laws No. U. z 2019 poz. 1881), - Act of 6 March 2018 on the rules for the participation of foreign entrepreneurs and other foreign persons in trade in the territory of the Republic of Poland (Journal of Laws). OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 994), - the Law of 12 April 2018 on rules for obtaining information on the non-criminality of applicants for employment and persons employed in financial sector entities (Journal of Laws No. Member of the Commission 1349), - the Law of 4 April 2019 on the promotion of scientific activities from the Polish Science Fund (Journal of Laws). OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 1060), - the Law of 25 February 2021 amending the Law - Bank law and certain other laws (Journal of Laws No. U. poz. 680).

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u493	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! First of all, I would like to thank you in the first words for the very good adoption of this proposal by the Committee on Public Finance and by Members from all sides of the political scene. As far as the burden of this project is concerned, both archiving, securing and building connections with the accounting systems offered on the market will be carried out on the administrative side, on the side of the solutions that will be implemented in the system at the time of its launch. At the beginning of October, workshops will begin with entrepreneurs who want to try this system and with companies providing accounting software so that these convenient connections with business systems can be implemented as soon as possible. As regards the technical issues related to the security, I would like to stress very strongly that this solution is proven in Italy, Portugal and Spain. In the country where it lasts the longest, in Italy, it's already been four years of operation of this system. There were no burglaries, no missing invoices. So that the technological solution itself is simply a safe, scalable solution implemented now in Poland. Both the technical parameters and the regulation itself have been the subject of not only our workshops with countries that have already implemented this solution, but also of consultation with the European Commission. Here we have, you can even say, a European quality certificate that confirms it. Poland would probably have to be the first country in which any irregularities would have occurred. With regard to the costs of implementing this system it is on the side of the Ministry of Finance in the first year it is about 130 million PLN. That's the cost of putting all the systems up. Later each year these expenses vary between PLN 70 million and PLN 80 million. These are the maintenance expenses of the system. As far as cost on the business side is concerned, it will actually result from the decision of the operators of financial and accounting systems. Given that this is a solution that is expected to become widespread among entrepreneurs since 2023, this solution will be scalable, it will be implemented in practically every tax and accounting system of each company. The lack of uniqueness of this service as well as the multiplicity of customers will naturally lead to the situation that this will not be an expensive solution. This will be implemented simply as part of the update of financial and accounting systems at the turn of 2022 and 2023. With regard to the question concerning the time limit for VAT refunds, VAT refunds are, of course, made, in monthly amplitudes, after the settlement of traders. Specific information about recent months will be available after we address specific departments of the National Tax Administration. We will answer this question in writing. In the case of amending invoices, there will be a new possibility from January to settle the corrective invoices in minus. First of all, in a situation where an adjustment has been made to the National e-Factur System, the party issuing the correction is not obliged to wait for the other party to approve the adjustment, which naturally facilitates the tax settlement of the entity making the adjustment. With regard to the withdrawal period for companies and giving them time to prepare for this on the one hand, already at this pilot stage companies will have a few months, October, November and December, to test the systems. The next year will be the year of application of this solution in the voluntary formula. There is no obligation here and there are no risks of the market simply not preparing for this solution. In total, there will be a year and 3 months to prepare the entire market for the implementation of this solution in the mandatory formula, so that we do not see here the risks associated with the possibility of implementing this solution in the following year. As far as the VAT gap and the sealing of the tax system are concerned, I would like to emphasise once again that the situation we are seeing now is diametrically different from what we saw 5 or 6 years ago. In 2015, in the pockets of the Mafia and in the grey zone, one in four zlotys was landed, which should go to the State budget for VAT. Now it lands there every tenth. This is at least PLN 20 billion a year more in the state budget. It is also 20 billion PLN, which does not land in the pockets of dishonest taxpayers, possibly tax offenders, and does not violate the competition rules between them and companies that fairly account for taxes. It is also a secret behind the fact that the Polish economy developed so quickly until the pandemic period and that it is a reflection of the post-pandemic planned for next year... Let me remind you that the projected GDP growth next year is between 4.5 and 5.7% by different credit rating agencies. So rapid regeneration and so good results of the Polish economy by 2019 are the result of this healing of relations between entrepreneurs and creating fair, safe conditions of competition for honest companies, so that companies can compete among themselves with quality of products, efficiency of running business, not dishonesty in paying taxes. The effects of sealing the tax system don't come out of nowhere either. This is the result of the legal changes introduced into the tax system and the true technological revolution. 2015 was the year of pencils, cards and rubbers. VAT settlements were carried out on paper. The first electronic declarations were introduced only a few years later. [[That's not true, Minister, it's not true.]] Now the whole reporting is implemented in the JPK formula. This allows automatic analysis of reports, compilation of transactions reported by both parties and tracking the movement of goods practically from their appearance in Poland to their departure to another country of the European Union. What's the national e-invoice system gonna do? It will give us that the analysis of trading between entities will be possible to begin much earlier, not after a month and 25 days - this is the longest possible date for starting the analysis - from the moment of the transaction and the issue of the invoice, and practically in real time. When the invoice is issued, information about this will be sent to the Ministry of Finance and analytical activity can be started immediately, which will reduce the life of the VAT carousel from 3 months to practically a few weeks. The result will also be that this VAT gap has a chance to decrease again by almost half: from 10% or 8% , also depending on the entity that analyses the VAT gap in Poland, to about 5% That is, the level that is now functioning in France. So that for the tax administration it's like changing from a bike to a fast bike. We want this new tool to increase the efficiency, productivity of the National Tax Administration, but above all the safety of entrepreneurs, to deliver it to these entrepreneurs as soon as possible. This is why there is an ambitious timetable for the implementation of this solution, but also a safe and, most importantly, based on proven solutions already implemented in other countries and accepted at European level. The remaining questions will be answered in writing. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u529	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! I was honoured to present a report on the work of the Committee on Energy, Climate and State Assets on 28 September 2021 on the Government's draft law amending the Act on the Functioning of Coal Mining and certain laws, print No 1503. The aim of the Act is to amend the legislation on hard coal mining. They will enable further restructuring and transformation of the hard coal sector in Poland and finance the effects of these processes. The aim of the amendments is also to allow the use of cover instruments and related social benefits by mining workers, mining plants or their designated part acquired from 1 December 2021 by the Company for the Restructuring of Mines. The new rules are a continuation of actions initiated by the government in 2015 following the adoption of a resolution plan for the Coal Company SA. They relate to the extension of the period of disposal by mining companies to the decommissioning company, as well as to the extension of the time limit allowing for the use of cover benefits by employees passing along with the property to the Company for the Restructuring of Mine. The need to continue restructuring the Polish hard coal mining sector is largely due to the long-standing difficult situation in the international coal market. At the same time, the draft law takes into account the provisions of the social agreement concerning the transformation of the coal mining sector and the selected transformation processes of the Śląskie voivodship, which was signed on 28 May 2021 in Katowice. The planned recovery plan aims to ensure the wider energy security of the State, as well as to continue coal mining economically justified and in line with the timetable contained in the social agreement. Failure to restructure and consequently the collapse of coal companies could result in very serious socio-economic repercussions for Upper Silesia. That's, among other things. the failure to fulfil financial obligations, the threat of national energy security, the social consequences of bankruptcy and the wider impact on coal-based co-operatives. The most important solutions proposed in the Act are the following. The company will be able to acquire further mines from operating coal companies and finance their liquidation from the state budget. These are mines whose decommissioning will start between 1 December 2021 and 31 December 2023. In addition, the draft law allows for further funding from the state budget of the tasks currently carried out by the Company for the Restructuring of Mines until the end of 2027. It was possible to benefit from cover instruments and social benefits payable to workers employed in the mine, mining plant or its designated part acquired by the SRK after 1 December 2021. This applies to persons who do not have pension rights. Support for workers will provide for mining leave or leave for workers of the mechanical processing plant or a one-off cash check. During the committee's work, seven amendments were tabled of an order, legislative and complementary nature. The Commission has given a positive opinion on the amendments to the Article. Point 3 (2), Article 4 (1) (a) (ii) (ii) (ii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) Other amendments concerning amendments to the provisions of Article The 1st committee gave a negative opinion. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commission recommends the draft law as it stands and requests its adoption. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u531	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! The draft law aims to carry out the restructuring of the hard coal sector in such a way as to mitigate its negative social impact and to provide adequate shielding instruments. The draft amending the law will have an impact on the situation and competitiveness of the Polish hard coal mining industry in view of reducing the volume of domestic coal supply on the market. In addition, mine decommissioning affects the environment by limiting the storage of post-production waste and stopping land degradation. However, the most socially significant consequence of the mining restructuring process is the reduction of employment in mining. According to mining companies' estimates, 1371 people will benefit from one-off cash checks and 2348 people will benefit from mining holidays, a total of 3719 people. At the meeting of the committee yesterday, the content of the bill was discussed, with the Legislative Bureau proposing several amendments of an editorial and legislative nature, which were agreed by the members of the committee. Several amendments made by the Left Club and the Civic Coalition were not accepted by the members of the committee. As a result, following the adoption of a number of, specifically, three parliamentary amendments, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice recommends that the High Sejm adopt the report of the printing commission No 1602. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u591	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! From this speaker I would like to remind you of another bloody pacification made by the German occupier in the area of the Thinking County during World War II. Here we are talking about the action carried out in September 1944 in Wiśniowa and Lipnik, in areas which became part of the so-called. The Republic of Raciechowicka. It was an area independent of the invaders, under the control of the troops and organizational structures of the National Army. In the whole Republic of Raciechowice during the pacification actions, the Germans murdered 132 people and burned 135 buildings. The first groups of conspirators composed mainly of former officers and reserve soldiers since the end of September 1939 began to secure weapons, ammunition and equipment left by the Polish Army. Soon, under the control and authority of the Polish Victory Service, and then the Polish Armed Forces and Home Army Association, there were other underground structures created in the area. In pre-war administrative division, the district of Thoughtnicki received a code name for Murawa. Since 1940, further field structures for intelligence, economic sabotage, arms storage and recruitment in underground structures of police officers, administrative representatives, etc. have continued to be established in the area. The military force of the circuit was initially called Murawa. a turf whose members, unlike the later guerrilla formations, were constantly in conspiracy. The first guerrilla troops appeared in the Thinking District in 1943. Soon the number of guerrillas, most of the deconspired members of the Their base became a forest complex in the Kamiennik and Lysina massif in the Thinking District. The pacification described in addition to Cherry and Lipnik also covered other villages. It was a manifestation of the Germans' brutality against the civilian population and powerlessness against the partisans of the National Army In the first hours of action, there was no indication that such a tragedy would occur. The German troops focused more on military purposes of the entire operation, that is, overcoming guerrilla groups holding control of the neighboring territories. At first, therefore, the Germans did not focus their attention on the civilian population. Some residents were hiding in nearby forests with some of their possessions. They were even calmed down by the Germans and encouraged to return to their homes. On Sunday, September 17th, the fire of buildings and the systematic arson of houses began. The Germans burned 9 farms and 23 more in the village of Lipnik in the pavilion of Czasław Żary. Another day, September 18th, was the most tragic. Early this morning, the Nazis launched a proper attack. A half kilometer wide belt has been designated, which has reached several villages. The gendarmes set up a tyralier and launched an attack on the defenseless people who were preparing for their daily duties. People were killed in their homes, bypasses and fields. At the time 77 inhabitants were murdered in Wiśniowa and 13 buildings burned down. In Lipnik, however, 126 buildings were consumed by fire and four people died. Among the victims were children, the youngest was 10 years old, and adults. The victims have been buried and are still resting in a cemetery in Wiśniowa. The martyrdom of the inhabitants commemorates a monument on a collective grave placed here. I pay tribute to this story to the memory of all the murdered inhabitants, as well as to the guerrillas of the National Army's district of Murawa, who led to the creation in 1944 of the local, independent and free territory of Poland - the Republic of Raciechowice. [[♪ The bell ♪]] This feat allowed the local people to feel the desire for freedom for a while. Thank you very much. Hello and glory to all the heroes and victims of that time! Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u593	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! His watches were worn by Albert Einstein, Walt Disney, Lew Tolstoy, Czar Nicholas II, Queen Victoria, Maria Skłodowska-Curie and the entire rich world. Of course, we are talking about Antoni Norbert Patku, a world-famous manufacturer of Swiss luxury watches, who, as life has proved, was born in the Sands of the Nobles in Lublin. This historical fact became known by chance - a few years ago professor of Lublin University of Technology Andrzej Wac-Włodarczyk, looking for information about his ancestors in the law firm of the parish of Tarnogóra, came across his birth certificate from 1812. Little Antoine, born of the father Joachim Patka of the coat of arms Prawdzic and the mother of Anna Piasecka, spends his childhood years in the Sands of Szlachecki and from here as a ten-year-old goes away with his family to Warsaw. At the age of 16, he joins the regiment of horsemen, taking active part in the November Uprising, where he is wounded twice. For his sacrifice and valor he earned the rank of lieutenant of the Augustan ride. As a 19-year-old, he is awarded the Golden Cross of the Order of Virtuti Militari. Following the fall of the uprising, the authorities go to Prussia and then to France, where they work as a zacer in the printing press. Soon he leaves for Switzerland and settles in Geneva. There he meets Franciszek Czapek, a participant of the November Uprising, with whom in 1839 he founded a manufacturer of artistic pocket watches. It uses its outstanding sense of entrepreneurship and artistic ability. In 1844 he met French watchmaker Adrien Philippe at a presentation in Paris. After a year Antoni Norbert Patek starts his own company in which he employs and then makes partner of the French watchmaker. A world exhibition in London in 1851, where Queen Victoria buys an ornamented brooch-shaped watch that she will now wear on her wardrobe, becomes crucial for the development of their joint company. But entrepreneurship is not the only work by Anthony Norbert Patek. It also carries out as a philanthropist in charity and aid activities. It helps Polish emigrants after the defeat of the January Uprising, and most of them employ them in their factory. He is also involved in Catholic activities, for which Pope Pius IX distinguished him with the Count's title. In business with their co-worker, they focus on innovation, and their products quickly gain global fame. In 1869 they build one of the first wrist watches in history. The brand is also famous for the fact that apart from the hours the watch shows, among others. Moon phase and days of the week. And the most expensive watch in the world is of course Patek Philippe's watch, sold at auction in Geneva, and quoted by an anonymous buyer for $31 million. Antoni Norbert Patek, although confessing his longing and ties with his homeland, remains in exile for the rest of his days. He dies on 1 March 1877 in Geneva at the age of 65 and is buried there too. And only sometimes the white and red colors on his tomb remind us of the roots. His company is sold out over time, but his founding name remains until today. Ladies and gentlemen! I am very pleased to announce that on 29 August this year. On the initiative of Andrzej Gołębie, director of the Regional Museum in Krasnystaw, and Lech Dziedzic, president of the Krasnostaw Historical Society, was restored the memory of Antoni Norbert Patk in public space, including in his home village, where he had a symbolic monument. On a huge stone extracted from his grandfather's field was placed in the very centre of Piast Szlachecki memorial plaque. The Swiss Deputy Ambassador Matthias Dettling, as well as representatives of the general government of all levels, church authorities and residents of the family village and surrounding area, came to the ceremony. An Antoni Norbert Patka's lifelike example prompts reflection, is the story and way of a little boy (The Bell) from one of the sublublin villages to the genius of the immortal fame of the Swiss company that the boy created. And we who have lived and are still alive... Thank you very much, Member. Mr. Marshal, just a second, because it's one sentence. ...so close to his home side, we are so proud of him. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u599	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Amendment of the Rail Transport Act of 24 March 2003 (Journal of Laws No. U. z 2020 pos. 1043) in Annex 1, which is a catalogue of elements of railway infrastructure exempt from real estate tax, has reduced the budgets of local government units. In connection with the principles of financial autonomy and tax autonomy of municipalities deriving from Article. 167 and 168 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland the loss of income requires compensation from another source or a possible reduction in the scope of public tasks to which the municipality is obliged. On 1 January 2017, the amendment of the Law of 16 November 2016 amending the Law on Rail Transport and certain other Acts entered into force (Journal of Laws No. U. z 2016 poz. 1923) amending the wording of Article. 7 Act of 12 January 1991 on local taxes and charges. The purpose of the amendment was to align national regulatory standards on rail transport with the amendment of European Union law, introduced by Directive 2012/34/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 21 November 2012 on the establishment of a single railway area and its implementing rules, as well as to amend the rules on rail transport of a sequencing and clarifying nature. These changes had a negligible impact on the financial situation of the municipalities. The catalogue of real estate tax exemptions was extended to include additional elements and included not only railway infrastructure buildings, but also buildings and buildings that are part of this infrastructure. This allowed for divergent interpretations. In the list of elements of the railway infrastructure, the components are defined in such a way that they include a number of elements which have not so far been considered as railway infrastructure, inter alia. land on which the infrastructure is located. The list of elements of the railway infrastructure shall include point 12 of the land marked as the registration parcels on which the elements listed in points 1 to 11 are located. It is sufficient, therefore, that any other element of the railway infrastructure is located on a given plot of land, so that the entire plot of land benefits from the tax exemption regardless of its area and regardless of its essential purpose. The experience from the four-year period of operation of the Act shows that it is necessary to compensate the government for losses in income and requires compensation for lost income. For example, due to the amendment of the Law on Rail Transport, the loss of the city of Braniewa in the years 2017 to 2021 is estimated at ca. 2670 thousand. zł. These are large amounts, especially for small municipalities, which, due to their number of inhabitants, cannot boast of high own influences. I appeal to the rulers to make these losses even. Further tasks and responsibilities continue to be imposed on local governments, but they do not go hand in hand with greater resources. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-29-sejm-38-1.u609	Declaration on The 40th anniversary of the establishment of the Independent Student Association. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Last week we celebrated a unique anniversary - it has been exactly 40 years since the first meeting of representatives of the NZS provisional founding committees at the student house of the Warsaw University of Technology took place. Following the adoption of the Statute, the Independent Student Association applied to the court for registration, which was rejected by the communist authorities. Thus began the largest student occupation strike in Europe. 47 requests included, among others, the release of political prisoners, the prohibition of admission to university premises of officers of the Civic Militia and the Security Service, the possibility of celebrating patriotic anniversaries, the freedom to choose foreign language learning, as well as the lack of coercion to learn political subjects, the reform of historical textbooks, university autonomy and, of course, the registration of the Independent Student Association, which finally happened in 1981. After the introduction of martial law, the Independent Student Association was delegated to transfer its activities to the underground. Currently it operates in 50 universities and counts about 5 thousand. Members. Thank you with all my heart to all the students who fought for free and democratic Poland.
